## Torlath Dynasty

Torlath was the ruling dynasty of Amn from the foundation of the realm in 460 DR to 1095 DR.

History
The dynasty was founded in the  by General Esmel, who seized the Emirate of Amin following the rapid collapse of the Shoon Imperium in 450 DR. Naming himself the King of Amn, he controlled the cities of Athkatla, Murann, and Crimmor, with the realm extending to Lake Esmel in the east. His grandchildren Imnel and Amnur proved capable and warlike men, and the royal line proceeded on with generally successful kings for centuries who expanded and enriched the realm.

The family faced a grave threat in the, however, when Arbas the Usurper slew almost the entire royal family including King Esmel IV and took the throne for himself. He was shortly after eliminated by the royal son Jhanos, who continued the much-winnowed family through a line of six "Wizard-Kings" who had long reigns, albeit disinterested ones that allowed the noble houses, merchant clans, and trade companies of the realm to gather increasingly large amounts of influence and power.

The Torlaths were finally obliterated between 1094 DR and 1095 DR. King Imnel IV was slain by assassins, followed by King Niskam the Doomed, King Rhor the Lackey, and King Dornak the Puppet King as the Braethar and Villax merchant houses attempted to control the throne. The Torlaths were wiped out, though the Wavegold family was able to claim a line of descent from the early royal Princess Jamyll and so took the throne for themselves in order to save the realm from a civil war.

The Tanislove family was actually a continuation from the Torlaths, though it was kept secret. The catastrophe of the Sothillisian War in 1370 DR convinced Pehllus Tanislove that the Council of Six was hopelessly corrupt and ineffective, leading to a conspiracy with the Knights of the Shield that would see the monarchy restored through the Tanisloves.

## Tome of the Soft-Step Trial

Tome of the Soft-Step Trial was a textbook used to teach Sharran initiates skills of stealth and daft hands, printed in the ancient times, long before the 15 century DR.

Content
The purpose of the Tome of the Soft-Step Trial was to help young initiates pass the Soft-step trial, part of the Gauntlet of Shar – a right of passage for Dark Justiciars of the Church. The tome instructed the readers on how to beat the trail using the skills known to be the most cherished among Sharrans – stealth, lockpicking, trap-disarming, and evasion. Only those who heeded the lessons of the ancient tome could hope to succeed.

## Torchriders

The Torchriders were an independent militia in the lands of southeastern Amn in the 14 century DR.

Activities
The Torchriders operated in the lands between the South Road of Amn and the Tethir Road, handling the countryside below the Tejarn Hills near Riatavin and Trailstone.

They were a loosely organized militia of mounted fighters that spread warning of attacks by raiders including goblins, hobgoblins, orcs, gnolls, and human deserters from the nearby Hillforts, and gathered up war-parties to respond to them in force.

History
The lack of a road between Hillfort Keshla and Riatavin meant a lack of overland trade, and the Council of Six determined that the area was unworthy of Council Army patrols. This lack of protection meant that bandits and warbands of humanoids were common, and the local farmers were forced to fend for themselves. Their response was the Torchriders, who allowed them to protect the region independent of the Council Army.

## Tomelia

Tomelia was the name given to a female gnoll archer who was mentally enslaved by a follower of the Absolute in the Moonrise Towers during the late 15 century DR.

History
Shortly before the, three gnolls had their will dominated by a True Soul cultist of a new fledgling religion—the Absolute. Linsella used her psionics, granted her via being infected by an illithid tadpole, to enslave and re-train the gnolls as her servants and cooks in the Moonrise Towers, which were the cult's base. She renamed her slave gnolls to Tomelia, Barnabus, and Timothy. Linsella claimed that she'd quelled the gnolls' savage nature and showed them a "gentler way." Tomelia was instructed to work in the kitchen, yet bloodthirstiness and rage still seethed underneath the painful mental domination.

When the gnolls and their mistress encountered a group of the True Soul heroes of Baldur's Gate they may had been helped by severing Linsella's mental leash, leading to the cultist's swift death. Some said that the heroes could've dominated the gnolls themselves, while by other accounts, the three gnolls could've remained under the cult's control and perished in an assault by Harpers and the True Soul heroes on the Moonrise Towers.

## Tombgate

Tombgate was a portal spanning Tsarn Tombs and Eveningstar.

Powers
One end of the gate was at Eveningstar Gorge just a little way from Eveningstar itself. It led to Tsarn Tombs near Scornubel.

History
In, Tessaril Winter took Shandril Shessair and Narm Tamaraith through the portal in disguise. They arrived at Tsarn Tombs near Scornubel, atop a hill, within sight of a road leading to a ferry.

The portal was known to the Zhentarim scriers Korthauvar Hammantle and Hlael Toraunt, who, by eavesdropping on a group of Highknights, confirmed Tessaril had went with two people through a portal, and sent a skull-token elsewhere. The portal was already known to them to have been used by Tessaril and two companions.

Tessaril later informed Lhaeo which portal she used and sent a skull token that would teleport one to the same location.

Rumors & Legends
Hlael Toraunt thought Hesperdan would know where Tombgate led.

## Torog

Torog, also called the King that Crawls, was an interloper god of death, the Underdark, imprisonment, and torture from the world of Nerath. Unlike other gods, Torog was imprisoned within the Underdark.

Abilities
On Nerath, Torog held dominion over the domains of earth and torment.

Divine Realm
According to the World Axis model, Torog resided in the Prime Material plane, as he was imprisoned in the Underdark.

Worshipers
Torog was one of the deities of the Dawn War, and Exandrian pantheons. He was worshiped by jailers and torturers, as well as many Underdark creatures.

Activities
Torog asked his worshipers to enjoy all kinds of pain, and to seek out and revere all the sacred places below the earth. He also opposed all kind of freedoms.

History
A short time before the Dawn War, Torog faced a terrible primordial of the Underdark on his own. While Torog was victorious and was able to kill it, the primordial managed to curse him, binding him to the Underdark and making him unable to heal from his wounds. Maimed and disfigured, Torog crawled across the Underdark searching for a way to the surface, breaking the dimensional boundaries that keep the Underdark separated from the Feywild and the Shadowfell in the process. Torog eventually managed to reach the surface world, but discovered that his powers were now tied to the Underdark and if he ever left it he would become powerless. Torog remained trapped in the Underdark ever since. Some people believed the World Serpent played a role in Torog's imprisonment.

As of the late 15 century DR, many surface folk incorrectly believed that Torog had faded away into myth; however, some people, like Elminster, reported that Torog was lurking in the Lowerdark below Faerûn.

Rumors & Legends
Some people believed that if someone spoke the name of Torog, the god would burrow from below and drag the speaker underground, where they would be imprisoned and tortured forever.

Content
The purpose of the Tome of the Self-Same Trial was to help young initiates pass the self-same trial, which was part of the Gauntlet of Shar and a right of passage for Dark Justiciars of the Church. Sharran acolytes had to muster the strength to defeat their and only mirror images. If a group took on a trial, each participant had to defeat their dark reflection, and those who attacked the others were penalized.

## Tower of Dracandros

The Tower of Dracandros was a tower located close to the village of Hap in Battledale. It was the home of Red Wizard of Thay, Dracandros of the Crimson, in the mid–14 century DR.

Location
The Tower of Dracandros was the latest keep built on the top of Haptooth Hill, a remnant of an ancient volcano. The hill helped the tower dominate the area and first of all the village of Hap. It was surrounded by a forest that was dense with oak, beech, and maple trees

Structure
The ground level of the Tower of Dracandros had a courtyard, a meeting hall, a festhall, and a kitchen. The meeting hall had an entrance to the caverns of Haptooth Hill, which led into the Underdark through a system of tunnels.

The floor above had a hall of triumphs, that is, a museum chronicling every noteworthy act Dracandros had engaged in. Trophies, mounted creatures, plaques, ribbons, and various certificates of recognition adorned the hall. The level also contained a meeting room with a huge portrait of the Thayan wizard.

The third level was used as a laboratory and a storage room for everything the wizard needed for his experiments.

Dracandros's bedroom and treasury were located on the fourth level of the tower. The treasury once had a manual of golems.

The flat roof of the tower used to be a place where the dragon minions gathered.

History
In the middle of the 14 century DR, the tower was built and became the residence of a Red Wizard of Thay, Dracandros of the Crimson, an ally of a dracolich named Crimdrac. Dracandros ruled the nearby village of Hap through the dracolich and his dragons, as well as with the help of the wizard's efreeti and drow minions. The Heroes of the Azure Bonds, among whom were Alias, Dragonbait, Olive Ruskettle, and Akabar Bel Akash, ended the terror of Crimdrac and Dracandros, and obtained the Helm of Dragons.

By 1371 DR, the tower was shattered, reduced to debris and decay, and surrounded by scattered dragon bones. The evil beings once residing in the tower were said to have finally been, slain leaving the tower empty. Nevertheless, the villagers of Hap avoided the hill and the tower. The most desperate adventurers visited the tower in pursuit of the Crown of Dracandros, but not all left the Wizard's Tower alive, being killed by its protective magic. The rest were content with collecting the dragon bones, which were in a great demand among alchemists and Sembians who wished to claim to be dragon-slaying heroes.

Inhabitants
 Dracandros: a mad wizard and the proprietor of the tower in the middle of the 14 century DR.
 Burly: Dracandros' personal guard.

## Toro-dai-be

Toro-dai-be was a village located in Miyama Province in Kozakura.

Description
Within Toro-dai-be, there were 68 buildings, including traditional minka homes, godowns, a mill, and a temple dedicated to the Toro-dai school.

Toro-dai-be was known for its unique characteristics. It lay within the boundaries of a Toro-dai shoen and was remarkable for the unwavering devotion of its inhabitants to the Toro-dai religious school. The villagers' commitment to their faith was so intense that they were willing to take extraordinary measures for its cause.

Government
As of, the village was led by a powerful shukenja who served both as the community leader and the manager of the shoen.

Defenses
Distinct from most villages, Toro-dai-be boasted a simple yet effective stockade for protection, complemented by a network of irrigation ditches and moats. While the village lacked samurai or bushi, it had a militia of 127 able-bodied men and boys. These individuals were armed with spears and naginatas and defended the village in times of crisis, such as during wars or frequent bandit raids.

Additionally, the village received external support in challenging times. During periods of conflict or at harvest, the provincial temple of the Toro-dai school dispatched twenty sohei to assist the villagers. These sohei played a crucial role in safeguarding the villagers and ensuring the safe transport of their agricultural rent to the relevant authorities.

## Too Much of Me Is Magic

Too Much of Me Is Magic is a one round adventure module set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in 2001, and is intended for adventurers of 5–13 levels.

Synopsis
During one summer evening, a group of heroes of the Living City was asked to embark on a political mission by the solar Aethylnmur of the Arrus demiplane. The group was tasked with escorting a new ambassador to the demiplane of Chertt; however, there was one catch, no powerful magical creatures could pass through the planar gate and no hero of the living City could pass with more than ten magic items without suffering harm. The passage back was only possible for the heroes carrying up to ten magic items. The group traversed the planes and escorted ambassador Diaphany to the Regent of Stone – the plane's ruler, and along the way, got involved with the so-called Big Boss – the corrupted ancient earth elemental named Bohuss, who sought to end the peace treaty between Chertt and Arrus.

## Torlathan

Torlathan was a city in eastern Amn that served as an important trade stop with the Dragon Coast, named after the royal Torlath family.

Geography
The city was built on the north bank of the Valashar River,  upstream of its confluence with the Khalieshyr.

History
Torlathan was an important part of the overland caravans between Amn and Westgate over the Giant's Plain. It was destroyed during the early Crusade of Slaughter in the, as the Bhaalist crusaders traveled from eastern Amn to Westgate along the trade route, crippling the east–west trade for decades. Attempts to rebuild the city and re-establish the trade routes proceeded slowly until the fall of the Amnian monarchy in the, when it was abandoned completely.

## Torch of revocation

Torches of revocation were a variety of enchanted torches that could also be used as maces in battle, invented sometime before the late 15 century DR.

Powers
Torches of revocation burned with pale green supernatural flames. When used as a weapon, they possessed a meager +1 enchantment to damage and guidance towards the target. Additionally, each attack with such a torch dealt additional necrotic damage and burned the targets with acid. Their main purpose, however, was in exorcism. The torches' flame destroyed anchors ghosts had on the Prime Material plane, banishing them for good.

History
Sometime in the, Oskar Fevras, a painter from Baldur's Gate, commissioned a torch of revocation from the Mystic Carrion, the mysterious mummy lord who offered his magical aptitude to the rich and powerful inhabitants of the Upper City of the Gate. The torch may have been used to burn the artist's portrait, haunted by his past lover scorned.

## Toysmiths

The Toysmiths was a renowned and prosperous toy shop in the city of Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR. It was owned and operated by a pair of gnome brothers, Gunder & Relvan Gaewilder.

Location
This shop was located Hearthston Hill, in the Mortonbrace Neighborhood of the Uptown District of Ravens Bluff.

Structure
This building stood a single story tall and had a window providing a view into its showroom. Overall the building didn't stand out a lot from the others that stood along Hearthston Hill. A brightly painted sign hung outside the shop, depicting a jester and bearing the words "TOYSMITHS" in multi-colored letters.

Interior

The front door of Toysmiths opened directly into its showroom, where all manner of toys covered the walls, crowded the corners, and even found room for display on some tables. A  tall counter also stood in the room for the brothers to stand behind. The only other door within the showroom lead into the offices of the shop, where business was conducted and all manner of paperwork was kept, such as correspondence with clients or invoices for materials. Within their office the Gaewilders kept a stock of various office supplies within their desk drawers, such as quills, inks, and paper.

Past the office room was the workshop proper, where the Gaewilder brothers crafted their toys. The rooms was a cluttered mess, with tables often covered in various tools and partially finished creations, as well as some magical supplies like oil of slipperiness or sovereign glue. Beyond the workshop was a kitchen, two bedrooms, and entryway to a connected stable that housed Toysmiths' two horses and its bright red horse cart.

The bed chambers of Relvan was decorated lavishly in bright colors, excluding anything red, and adorned with a great deal of toys. By comparison, the room of Gunder was rather spartan in appearance. Along the west wall was a large metal depiction of the symbol of Flandal Steelskin, a fiery hammer, where he would pray for his divine spells. Within a small desk lit by oil lamp Gunder kept books on craftsmanship, religion, and gnome history. In the desk's bottom drawer was kept a small iron lockbox, trapped by Gunder with a electrical glyph of warding, that stored the business's profits.

Every night Relvan set up a rudimentary security system in the showroom. This consisted of a tripwire across that ground that, when triggered, would cause four toy soldiers wielding little crossbows to fire darts laced in a paralytic poison. Additional doses of this same poison were kept in a vial by Gunder within his bedroom.

Services
Toysmiths specialized in selling a wide variety of different toys that were all made on site, catering to both commoners and the rich. The shop also took commissions and sold some products for resale to Traagor's Tours and Souvenir Shop. Their products included such things as rag dolls, a wide variety of fabric dolls that were stuffed with either down or felt, porcelain dolls, bow and arrow sets with five play arrows, rocking mounts, wheeled animal models, and leather balls ranging from  in size. The shop also carried two notable lines of action figures.With some exceptions, most of these toys outlined in Tears of Selûne match up with ones previously detailed in Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.

The Avatar series was a line of 55 figures that each depicted one of the known deities of the Realms. Out of these fifty five, three were different versions of Bane and two were different versions of Torm. Figures could be purchased individually for 8 silver pieces, or they could all be bought together as a full set for 20 gold pieces. The other notable line of figures was the Heroes of the Realms,It is left unclear in this case if "Gods of the Realms" meant the line depicted deities from all across Toril or was limited solely to the known gods of Faerûn. a line of clay figures depicting various renowned adventurers. Each figure was custom made and sold for 3 gold pieces.

Among the most expensive of the Gaewilder products, as well as the most sought after by adults of nobility, were their intricate clockwork toys. Crafted of gold and inlaid with precious gemstones for eyes, these clockworks were typically made in the form of birds or other small animals.

Activities
Though the business was quite prosperous, it frequently had surpluses of unsold toys. It was for this reason, according to the Gaewilder brothers, that several times a year they would load up their horse cart with the surplus stock they didn't have room for and ride through the city to give the toys out. This was usually done during a festival or holiday. Both of the Gaewilders would dress themselves up in bright makeup and colorful costumes for the occasion. And Relvan would go a step further by putting on a performance of dancing, juggling, and acrobatic tricks to delight the onlookers.

History
Circa 1341 DR,In The Swearing Stone, an adventure estimated to take place in 1371 DR because it's set 13 years after the Time of Troubles, it is stated that the Gaewilder brothers have been in business for thirty years. This would put the opening of their shop at roughly 1341 DR. on either Eleasis 1 or Shieldmeet,A lot of the events in the adventure The Swearing Stone center around a Shieldmeet celebration that occurred two weeks prior, which just so happened to fall on the anniversary of the opening of Toysmiths. The Swearing Stone takes place in 1371 DR, however, Shieldmeet only occurs once every 4 years and it's known from other material that a Shieldmeet celebration had occurred in the year 1372 DR. If one were to assume that Ravenians celebrate Shieldmeet regardless of year or that this was a special occurrence, then the actual date would likely be Elasias 1. Alternatively, if one were to assume that the adventure's date is wrong and should be 1372 DR, then Toysmiths was instead opened on Shieldmeet in 1340 DR. Toysmiths was established by the Gaewilders after Gunder left the Church of Flandal Steelskin in response to being ridiculed by his fellow Flandalians for his focus on toysmithing.

For a time it was considered quite fashionable among Cormyrean nobles to gift King Azoun Obarskyr IV one of the intricate clockwork toys crafted by the Gaewilder brothers of Ravens Bluff, until Azoun himself grew tiresome of the fad and would sigh loudly whenever he was presented with another.

In 1369 DR,The article about the Toysmiths in Polyhedron #50 states that the Toysmiths began 28 years prior. Taking into account the prior dating estimates, that would put the "Present" of that article at 1369 DR. the earnings of the Toysmiths totaled at 4,346 gold pieces. Around that time the Gaewilders notably had in their possession two gold lion figurines of wondrous power of their own creation, though Gunder would try to take sole credit for their creation whenever his brother wasn't present. The Gaewilders sought to sell the figures for no less than 5,000 gold pieces, but chose not to publicly advertise them, only showing them off to customers who inquired about magic items and looked like they could afford the steep cost. For safekeeping these figurines were kept within an iron lockbox, trapped by Gunder with a glyph of warding that would cause electrical shocks, and kept within one of their office desk's drawers.

In 1371 DR, on a Shieldmeet celebration, the Gaewilders celebrated the 30 anniversary of their business together by making a pledge at the famed Swearing Stone to continue their partnership for another thirty year. Two weeks later, Unbeknownst to them and others in Ravens Bluff, a planar rift was allowing the plane of Limbo's chaotic energies to seep in and corrupt the landmark, causing all those who had made pledges to begin behaving dishonestly and fall back on their promises.

This chaos corruption made Relvan start to think he wanted out of toysmithing, driving him to sell the shop to an interested buyer and lie that his brother was dead. An equally mind corrupted Gunder was outraged by his brothers actions and decided to hold him hostage within the store until either his Relvan changed his mind or he could convince the interested buyer in backing out of the deal. He adhered Relvan to a table with sovereign glue, tied his hands together with ribbon, scattered jacks and marbles across the floor, and wrapped around his nose the tripwire for his nightly toy soldier trap. He also barred the front door to their shop and had their guard dog sat on the table beside his brother.

A large crowd came to gather outside the shop to gawk at the situation. Lieutent Veriway of the City Watch's I&N Team, lacking resources to check out the premises, tried to negotiate Relvan's safe release. Eventually a group of adventurers investigating the Swearing Stone came by the shop and overheard Gunder talking about the anniversary meeting. Gunder then made two demands in return for his brother's safe release, that he be not sent to prison and has Toysmiths returned to him.

The adventurers would manage to talk him down into releasing his brother, but the moment Gunder left the store Veriway sneered at him, calling the gnome an "idiot" that wasn't going to get his store back. He then called upon fellow guardsman to arrest Gunder, proclaiming he was going to be sent away to the Golden Ball. Some of the adventurers tried to protest his arrest, but Veriway told them to take it up with Lord Magistrate Tordon Sureblade.

Inhabitants
Both brothers equally contributed to the crafting of toys. Relvan, the younger of the two brothers, was arguably the better craftsman and put the finishing touch on almost everything they made. He was disinterested in most business details though and trusted his brother Gunder to keep track of all such matters, including record keeping and the holding of the Toysmiths's earnings.

Besides the brothers themselves, the store was inhabited by a animated skeleton of a war dog. This creature had been reanimated by Gunder and put inside of a large stuffed dog, acting as a covert guard animal. It typically stood within the showroom, near the door to office during business hours and in the middle of the showroom at night or whenever both brothers were away from the shop. Inside the stables of the Toysmiths were a draft horse dyed blue, named Sir Blue, and a pony named Hazelnut. The Gaewilders usually kept around  sacks of high quality oats for the two.

Reputation
The Toysmiths was well beloved by many generations of Ravenians, so much so that any attempts to burglarize or vandalize the shop would swiftly be met with mass intervention from the locals. Even if one could manage to steal from the shop, its merchandise was so distinctive that it could easily be traced back to the thieves who fenced it.

The masterful craftsmanship of the the brothers also made Toysmiths renowned far beyond the confines of Ravens Bluff and the Vast, being known as far away as Cormanthor and Rashemen. Both Sembian and Waterdhavian nobles frequently paid Toysmiths large sums of gold for its clockwork toys. And tales of their craftsmanship caused travelers from even farther afield to visit Ravens Bluff to get a look at the Gaewilders' work.

## Tooralooralayo

Tooralooralayo was a human woman with a light skin tone, dark brown eyes, and shortly-cropped blonde hair. Her skin was dirty and clothes were humble.

History
In the, Spatula Farggo, a worker from the Elfsong Tavern's kitchens, began helping out the beggars in the Lower City, by bringing them kitchen scraps and leftovers to those in need, both locals, and the refugees fleeing fro the armies of the Absolute. Tooralooralayo and her son, Pickles, were among those who happily accepted the scraps. Tooralooralayo's boy was happy to have more than one meal a day and the woman herself worked about Spatula Farggo getting in trouble for his sneaky generosity.

## Toasted bread

Toasted bread or simply toast, was one of the simplest handfoods found in the Realms. It consisted of cooked bread, sometimes seed-bread, often smothered in spreads or soaked in sauces.

Description
Toasted bread was often eaten topped with soft butter, jam, or gravy, and could be prepared on the road over campfire flames. Specialized toasting implements were also used in the coastal town of Spandeliyon in Altumbel. These toasting irons were used to affix bread and cheese together to be fire-roasted.

It was also known to be served as a side for various dishes, like bowls of simple broth, or plated among morningfeast foods like eggs, ham, bacon, apples, and melons, or topped with sliced fried sausage, or stirge, like they did in Waterdeep and Suzail. The dish called tharval-and-dleem was a spread made with tharval, a type of tiny fish, mashed and smothered over toasted bread. Guests of The Jester's Pride in Julkoun could try another fish variation of toast – topped with river trout with a sauce of lemon, cream, and pepper. Tabra's festhall and The Riverman inn in Blackfeather Bridge served buttered toast topped with mushrooms and oysters to its guest and the simple dish was recommended by Volothamp Geddarm as a highlight of the menu.

Toast was also known to be a simple dessert treat when it was served crusted with sugar, notably seen in the Feywild, or covered in butter and cinnamon, as was popular among the guests of The Copper Cup in Waterdeep.

History
During his visits to Earth, Elminster and the the Wizards Three imbibed squares of toast, something that was presumed to be a type of bread from that world.

## Tombstone

The Tombstone was the fortified central keep of Surcross in Thay, and was known by this name as of the late 15 century DR. It served as both the seat of power for the Thayan military governor and as the final line of defense if the city came under attack.

Structure
It was a squat and ugly stone tower that was the most heavily reinforced structure in Surcross.

Interior
The interior walls were covered in runes. The keep included a courtroom known as the Hall of Petition as well as a modest torture chamber with manacles on one wall. The cellar contained a vault that was defended by skeletal guardians.

Zulkir's Chamber
At the top of the Tombstone was a grand chamber decorated much more opulently than the rest of the keep, and which was a perfect replica of an audience chamber in the Citadel of Szass Tam. It contained a golden throne flanked by heavy curtains and faced by a small amphitheater, and the whole area was lit by braziers that needed no kindling and which exuded a peculiar transparent smoke. The twin chambers were linked such that individuals in either one could open a line of communication to the other, causing illusions of everyone in the other room to appear and be able to converse. This was used by Szass Tam and his emissaries to give orders to and receive reports from Surcross. The chamber in the Citadel additionally had the power to teleport anyone on the Tombstone side into the Citadel.

Defenses
The main gate could disappear by melding into the stone walls if the keep came under assault. The internal walls were enchanted.

If needed, the Tombstone could be reinforced instantly by Thayan soldiers arriving via teleportation circles from the city's Garrison.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, the military governor of Surcross was Ukulsid, who occasionally held court in the Hall of Petition to hear citizens' grievances and reported to Thay via the Zulkir's Chamber based on a schedule. He also made use of the torture chamber: by far his most frequently used means of torture was to have his spellcasters regenerate a victim's flesh as it was continually devoured by ghouls in a practice known as the "endless feast". Torture was used both to interrogate captured enemies as well as to punish disobedience.

Around the, Valindra Shadowmantle was using the vault in the Tombstone's cellar as an ossuary to store the bones of dragons, which she hoped to use to create dracoliches. Only she and her minions were permitted access to this vault.

## Tome of the Stilled Tongue

The Tome of the Stilled Tongue was a heinous spellbook created by a treacherous servant of Vecna. There existed five copies of the tome, though which of them was the original was unknown.

Description
These books were thick volumes bound in leather, each grisly decorated with a dessicated tongue nailed to their cover. The first few pages of each tome were taken up by indecipherable script. The tongue on the first copy of this tome belonged to the treacherous priest of Vecna, who was terrible at keeping secrets, whilst the tongues on the other four copies belonged to spellcasters that had crossed Vecna. These tongues could only be removed by those attuned to the book and once done it would permanently erase all spells written within it.

Powers
Each copy of the Tome of the Stilled Tongue contained variety of wizard spells, instructions for creating a lich's phylactery, and eldritch mental exercises for improving one's spellcasting abilities. There were also 500 blank pages that one could write their own spells in. These pages functioned similarly to those found within a copy of Boccob's blessed book, meaning that costly materials were not necessary in order to inscribe spells upon its pages. Additionally, the book could function as a spell focus and could be used to cast spells without the need of somatic or verbal components.

Originally these tomes could only be utilized by worshipers of Vecna, but following the Second Sundering it was found that any spellcaster could utilize them. However, unbeknownst to those who wielded these books, Vecna was able to spy on them and send them cryptic messages within the pages of a tome. These messages would show up at around midnight and disappear shortly after being read.

Crafting
Crafting a copy of the Tome of the Stilled Tongue required not only knowledge of how to craft wondrous items and sanctify relics, but one had to have the spell secret page and be a worshiper of Vecna.

Notable Owners
 Lahnis, a Red Wizard of Thay.

## Tourmaline Depths

The Tourmaline Depths were a subterranean expanse occupied by the ruins of an unknown origin beneath Baldur's Gate leading to the Underdark.

Structure

The upper levels
The rapier Pelorsun Blade which had "sliced Kas the Bloody-Handed's throat" and its last possessor were buried here and remained intact until 1492 DR.

The upper levels of the Tourmaline Depths were used as Cazador Szarr's prison where he held vampire spawn and slaves.

The Grand Chamber was used for rituals.

History
Amanita Szarr retold the known facts she had read in the writings of master vampire Donnela the Architect. According to Amanita's book, The Tourmaline Depths, during the period between 1138 and 1204, Donella reopened the sprawling blue-green halls beneath Baldur's Gate, placing them under the Szarr family's control. Donella suggested that dwarven exiles from the city of Bhaerynden were the original builders of the Tourmaline Depths, which seemed to serve as an outpost or a mining facility rather than a city. Still, by 1492 DR, it remained a mystery what resources the dwarves were extracting. It was also puzzling how these long-abandoned depths had stayed hidden beneath a major city like Baldur's Gate for so long.

After killing vampire lord Vellioth the Martinet, Cazador Szarr succeeded him as master vampire of Baldur's Gate in . Cazador made one of the upper rooms his personal bedroom and placed Vellioth's skull on a pillow on a pedestal there.

In, Mordoc SeLanmere, the Pale Knight, used the Onyx Heart to transform the citizens of Baldur's Gate into zombies under his control, while his vampires transformed other citizens into vampires. The city was temporarily renamed to Mordoc's Gate. By 1492 DR nothing was known of the relations between Mordoc and Cazador Szarr during the conquest of Baldur's Gate, but it was possible that Cazador assisted Mordoc, as he had a bust of Mordoc SeLanmere in Szarr Palace. the Tourmaline Depths could have been somehow used by Mordoc.

In, Cazador Szarr performed the Black Mass Ritual, but was stopped by the Absolute Adversaries.

## Tombstone golem

Tombstone golems were a deathtouched variant of stone golems with their bodies composed of the stone of various fused together tombstones and necrotic energy.

Abilities
These constructs lacked a stone golem's ability to slow other creatures, but in its place had a supernatural ability comparable to the spell slay living. Additionally, they could inflict baneful doses of necrotic energy upon living creatures they touched. And if destroyed, their bodies would violently explode, sending out shards of stone and a burst of necrotic energy.

Combat
Much like a standard stone golem, these constructs relied heavily upon their fists to harm the opposition.

Society
Construction
On average, building a tombstone golem required the spell slay living and around approximately 70,000 gold pieces.

History
Around, a group of adventurers traveled through a portal beneath the city of Myth Drannor, taking them into Shazryl's tower in the Shadowfell. There they encountered a great number of creatures under the alhoon's command, including a trio of forsaken shells that guarded a room alongside a tombstone golem.

## Tower of Seven Woes

The Tower of Seven Woes was a structure on Skull Island in Skullport.

Location
The Tower of Seven Woes was hewn from a massive stalactite, which appeared as though it pierced the upper halls of the prison at the southern end of Skull Island.

Structure
The tower derived its name from the seven levels carved into the heart of the stalactite, each symbolizing a particular form of suffering in devotion to Loviatar, the goddess of pain and suffering. While the details of the upper six levels were kept secret, the first was known as the Agony of Frost and Fire.

Activities
The Tower of Seven Woes was regarded with even greater fear than the notorious School of Obedience.

It was the site where Quinan Varnaed, the leader of the Iron Ring, conducted rituals dedicated to his deity, Loviatar. These services, steeped in blood and agony, were held with the assistance of four additional priests, though they operated discreetly to avoid drawing the attention of the Skulls of Skullport. On holy days dedicated to Loviatar, the tower resounded with the screams of the tormented, their cries echoing across the Port and striking fear into all who heard them.

Defenses
The Tower of Seven Woes was defended by a contingent of gargoyles and margoyles that patrolled the skies around the stalactite. They were magically bound to obey Quinan Varnaed, serving as guardians, scouts, and even personal messengers. Rivals of Varnaed had, on more than one occasion, been attacked and carried into the air by them, only to be delivered to the cruel mercies of the tower's master.

## Tower Threespires

Tower Threespires was an overgrown wizard tower in the eastern edge of the Dunwood in the Great Dale.

Structure
At a distance, this ruined wizards tower looked like a giant three-fingered hand reaching out of the Dunwood towards the sky. The three towers stabbed out from a broad-based construction of stone, which would've collapsed many years previously if not held together by a great network of vines wrapping themselves around every inch of the three towers. Were the vines to wither and lose their cohesion, the entire tower complex would come crashing down. The peak of the north tower contained a portal to Clymph Tower.

History
The Nar demonbinder Clymph built this tower in the, two years after creating his fortress of Clymph Tower. He linked the two by a permanent portal, making this the only way to enter or leave the sealed Clymph Tower.

Inhabitants
By the late 14 century DR, the tower was inhabited by a small group of druids, all descendants of those chosen personally by the Nentyarch leader of the Circle of Leth, to be part of a special order of guardians several centuries earlier. They were to protect the lands and forests against the demons held in Clymph Tower. Every year on the Feast of the Moon, they performed a ritual to maintain the portal seal, preventing any trapped demon from slipping out into the Great Dale. While the druids were no longer sure if the ritual was necessary or whether any demons still lingered in Clymph Tower, they nonetheless still performed their appointed task of securing the Tower and Portal both.

Long isolation had made the druids suspicious of strangers, never allowing anyone else to enter the tower, no matter how dire the situation, in fear of the portal unsealing and thus their duty to fail.

## Tower of the High Wizard Netheril

The Tower of the High Wizard Netheril was a mage's tower in Freedale in the Dalelands.

Location
The tower lay out of the way, but still close to Freedale.

Structure
The tower had three floors.

Interior
Each of the tower's floors was a single room holding no end of arcane implements: books, notes, and glassware. The first floor held his office, while the rest were private chambers: the second floor was sleeping quarters, the third floor was storage, and the fourth was his secret laboratory. However, there were a number of rooms cluttered with arcane implements on each floor.

Services
Inside, Netheril cast spells for those who paid, and sometimes sold minor magic items or spells.

Defenses
Three guards protected the tower: Major Domo, Fortu, and Deval.

History
In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Netheril called upon local adventurers to rescue Taran Goldenstar from the Tomb of Damara near Freedale. He met them at his tower. If they succeeded, he may have asked them afterwards to find and recover the orb of dragonkind, again at his tower.

Appendix

The tower is adapted from First Quest, a Mystara starter kit for AD&D, where a wizard "Nethril" has a nigh-identical tower.

## Tome of Ascendance

The Tome of Ascendance was an artifact spellbook that once belonged to the agents of the Cloaked Ascendancy during their assault on Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR.

Powers

When a mage cast spells from the Tome of Ascendance, they were changed and gained additional properties. One of the four changes happened: whatever the spell was, it left behind an area of effect of four types – an aboleth, a fey, a fireforged, or an illusion. When a friendly creature passed through one of these areas, they were consumed by the book's magic, granting the wielder of the Tome of Ascendance a special ability depending on the area that consumed a creature. If the area of effect was an aboleth, all of the wizard's attacks, be they physical or magical, gained the ability to spread plague; if it were fey, the mage received less damage, their movement speed increased, and they healed; if the effect was a fireforged, all creatures around the mage were enveloped in chains and pulled towards them whilst the mage's attacks, both spells and physical, were boosted; and lastly, if the area was an illusion, the caster's speed was boosted and they received the boon of becoming stealthy and hid from their enemies.

History
The Tome of Ascendance was recovered from the wreckage of Neverwinter's Blacklake District in the late 15 century DR. It was brought there by one of the wizards of the Cloaked Ascendancy and was believed to hide a number of dangerous and exotic spells.

Wearing a robe of blending, a ring of regeneration, and a pair of bracers of defense he fought with two enchanted Sang kauws. In his possession he also had 5 potions of healing.

Relationships
His brother was once another samurai but their paths diverged once Okuma went mad and went on a killing spree during a fit of rage.  Unknown to Toraga he was being actively hunted down by his brother, as Okuma had put all his insane blame over his disgrace on Toraga.

## Toarin Klustoon

Toarin Klustoon was a slyblade assassin for the Zhentarim in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was a small, agile man.

Personality
Though a killer, he cared about his friends.

Abilities
He executed notably complex assassination maneuvers, even in combat with other stealthy operatives.

Possessions
He carried strangling wire and a dagger.

Relationships
He was friends with Strabbin Stillcorn and Rungo Baerlan, fellow Zhentarim.

History
Toarin Klustoon was one of Calivar Murpeth's people in Warmfires House during a night revel in the . A woman they captured, Talantha, claimed she'd only helped the scribe Maervidal Iloster escape the revel after his poisoning because she was threatened by Blaeragh Ridranus, another Zhentarim slyblade. Toarin slew him after a brief struggle. She then fingered four other operatives as having helped Ridranus. Those were killed by fellow slyblade Taerach Thone; Toarin became restless at that point, and demanded Talantha be more forthcoming, as he affected certainty Strabbin Stillcorn and Rungo Baerlan were not traitors. The woman soon revealed herself to be Storm Silverhand in disguise, and killed him.

## Tourmaline golem

Tourmaline golem were a weaker constructs similar to the emerald gemstone golems found in the nation of Thay, created by the Red Wizard Marcos Flass in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Tourmaline golems were construct creatures fashioned using tourmaline gemstones, mined from the Termalaine mines in the Ten Towns of Icewind Dale. Despite them being weaker and overall inferior variety of gemstone golems, they were still, nevertheless, dangerous foes.

History
Tourmaline golems were created in the, by Marcos Flass, a disgraced Thayan Red Wizard. When the town of Termalaine was emptied out by an unnatural blizzard and an army of verbeegs, the town's famous mines were abandoned and Marcos Flass seized that opportunity, hoping to use the mine's tourmaline to redeem his good name in the far-away Thay. He managed to quickly develop and build a batch of golems, when the Hero of Neverwinter found their way into the Flass laboratory during their operation to free Termalaine from the invading force. Marcos may have had negotiated with the Hero and their companions, allowing him to finished the golemcraft work in exchange for loaning the Ten Towns his freshly-carved tourmaline golem in case there was a battle to come against the demigod Arctus and his army of creatures of the tundra. The battle did commence soon after, however, it was unknown if the deal was made, securing the gemstone golems' aid in the life-and-death battle.

## Tonthur-nut sauce

Tonthur-nut sauce was a condiment made out of tonthur nuts harvested from the mountainous areas in Amn, Tethyr, near Starmantle, Turmish, and the Vilhon Reach, often used to give flavor to bland foods like potatoes or cover up the cook's mistakes.

Description
To make the sauce, tonthur nuts were crushed and then boiled with a number of spices. The resulting sauce was a thick brown condiment with mouth-sizzling spicy heat. The sauce also inherited the heavy body from the natural fats that tonthur was fulled with. The sauce also had a nutty, peppercorny, and mushroomy flavor.

The sauce was packed in small clay jars. Their tops were sealed and, once the sauce was used up, the jars were used to store oils and other similar substances.

History
By the 15 century DR, tonthur-nut sauce was sold by merchant caravans and drovers all along the Sword Coast, the Tashalar, and anywhere else the Calishite merchants traveled.

To'Ming was a trade city in Shou Lung that was part of the Ch'ing Tung Province.

Geography

## Torin Betrich

Torin Betrich was a peasant of Eveningstar, Cormyr, in the late 15 century DR.

History
During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, Torin's friend Ana Brabener was captured by the drow along with several other villagers of Eveningstar during their hunt for the Thread of the Weave and were held in the Eveningstar Cavern. As the captives were about to be dragged into the Underdark, adventurers from Eberron emerged into the cavern from the portal to the Underdark and slew Ana's drow captors, freeing her and the other villagers.

After Ana's rescue, Torin chose to search for the Thread of the Weave to find it before the drow did. Ana, along with their mutual friend Cormul Haythorne, had initially decreed it to be too dangerous. However, they later relented and joined Torin, seeking out an old Temple of Mystra deep in the King's Forest. However, the three were ambushed by the drow when they neared the temple. At that moment, Ana manifested an unknown power, resulting in an explosion that killed several of the drow and knocked out Cormul and Torin. Due to her strange display of magic, she was captured by Odezzt Avithoul and taken deep into the temple, where the taskmaster sought to discover how it was that she had cast that spell. After they recovered, Cormul returned to Eveningstar to find help while Torin remained in the woods to search for Ana.

## Tommy Fletcher

Tommy Fletcher was a little shepherd boy of Stag Falls in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was a small boy.

Possessions
He had a little lamb.

Activities
He retrieved stray lambs.

History
About a month after the population of Stag Falls had fled for the nearby caves because of the sudden insanity of Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls, who had been turned into an undead thrall by Romanisk Vlaensk, an ancient wizard and vampire, Tommy Fletcher pursued a little lamb that fell off a cliff towards a location close to town. There, Tommy himself fell off a cliff, getting a life-threatening, compound fracture. He was fortunately spotted by adventurers sent from Ravens Bluff, to which taxes were owed by Stag Falls but had been disrupted by the commotion. Young Fletcher helped the adventurers, leading them to the caves where the population had hid away, after they healed him.

## Totem animal

A totem animal was a spirit-like creature resembling an animal that was encountered by priests known as skinwalkers during their solitary meditations in the woods. These meditations, undertaken as part of their path to becoming skinwalkers, granted the enlightenment and wisdom necessary to assume the form of their totem animal. This magical power was known as skinwalking.

They could be sought out by others for different purposes. For example, they could be asked to show support or undertake a sort of patronage over an individual that tried to show their worthiness to stay on Evermeet.

The Tower was a tower on Isle Broddick in the Whamite Isles. It was a key defense of Isle Broddick in a series of wild conflicts between various nations and pirates of the Whamite Isles at some point prior to the mass death of the population in the .

Magic users like the Wizard of Broddick as well as Silidon and his apprentice, were able to command the Tower at its full power and increase its defenses significantly.

## Tormos Ollday

Tormos Ollday was a human merchant of Tulmene, Tethyr, and the guildmaster of the Guild of Fishmongers and Fishermen there in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Perhaps the most unscrupulous merchant in town, Tormos engaged in all manner of illicit enterprises and dealt with the Rundeen, the Shadow Thieves, and pirates. However, he kept most of this behind various front businesses, so he maintained a reputation as a respectable and legitimate businessman who'd brought prosperity to this guild for three decades by 1370 DR. Should the guildmembers—mostly hardworking and honest folk—discover the truth, they would punish him severely.

## Tombli

Tombli was a half-duergar dwarf warcaster of Abbathor, and the captain of the Company of the Chimera in the late 14 century DR.

Description
The dwarf's fingers were blistered as was his forge-hardened face. He had hard, black eyes.

Personality
Tombli was domineering and sadistic.

Abilities
He was a warcaster of Abbathor, a sort of cleric, though his healing was inferior.

One of his spells caused veins to burst through the skin, flaying it; he named it Abbathor's flowering.

Possessions
He bore a bejeweled dagger, likely a dagger of venom, that served also as his holy symbol.

Activities
Tombli led the Company of the Chimera as its captain.

History
Tombli was the child of an exiled dwarf and a duergar princess; he was born an outcast. He eventually became the leader of the Company of the Chimera.

He'd once skinned a woman alive.

Years later, in Mirtul 21 of the, he got into a fight with Saskia, a recalcitrant Uthgardt woman who had joined his company. He was losing the brawl until his own men stepped in to hold her down. Later that day, he purchased a pseudodragon from a Calishite trader; he had to ask Saskia to identify the creature. The dragon struck her with its venomous stinger in the process.

He had its stinger amputated afterwards, and tormented it in the stables through the day. Come night, he was ambushed by Saskia again. Though he had the upper hand at first, paralyzing her with magic and torturing her, she displayed a previously unseen talent for magic and unlocked the cage telepathically. The furious pseudodragon made him flee into the night. His men then went after Saskia.

## Toramir Kuurgan

Captain Toramir Kuurgan was a former Tethyrian naval officer and later leader of the Sea Reavers pirate fleet of the Nelanther Isles in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
Toramir Kuurgan was once a commander of Tethyr's royal navy. But amidst the turbulent Tethyrian Interregnum after 1347 DR, he felt betrayed by the nobility for their selfishness and therefore betrayed his fallen kingdom. Circa 1350 DR, Toramir reassembled his flotilla with four ships and 200 crewmembers and officers loyal to him, and permitted safe passage back to the Dragon's Neck peninsula for any who refused and remained allegiant to the navy—only four departed. He joined his newly established fleet with the survivors of the old Sea Reavers and operated them as pirates for the next two decades. In time, their fleet grew to five vessels.

The Sea Reavers were amongst those Nelantheran pirate groups who held sway over the leaders of Tulmene, a small shipbuilding town on the Dragon's Neck. For some twenty years, they made the place a secret safe harbor for their kind. But in Eleasias of the, the newly acclaimed warrior-queen of Tethyr, Zaranda Star, arrived in Tulmene on her tour of the realm and soon discovered the truth. She and her band rallied the townsfolk and battled the pirates at the harbor, finally driving them out to sea. Two of the Sea Reavers' ships were damaged in the fighting, and when they were forced to enter the Race and its intense winds and currents, they were sunk. Incensed by the loss of ships and crewmen and corrupted by two decades of crime, Toramir had no desire to rejoin in the reformation of Tethyr and instead swore vengeance against the new queen, even to see her dead.

The Sea Reavers encountered difficulties supplying and maintaining their remaining ships and so in the late 1360s DR Toramir made a deal to work for the Rundeen mercantile consortium.

But with Tethyr's restoration in the, and a rebuilt strong and capable navy, pirates like the Sea Reavers found its shipping too hard a target and so turned to prey upon that of Amn. In late Eleint, the Sea Reavers attacked and looted four ships of a seven-strong fleet belonging to the Vemmil family of Amn, which were returning from a voyage to Zakhara. The other three made it to Murann. Some time the following year, under the auspices of the Rundeen, Toramir made an alliance with the ogre mage Sythillis of the Sythillisian Empire to blockade Amnian ports like Murann to prevent treasure ships fleeing, in exchange for a cut of that treasure, as well as stop the Amnians retaking the city. They hoped to acquire ships and a new safe port on the Sword Coast. By 1372 DR, the Rundeen and their pet Nelanther pirates like Toramir were using Murann as a safe haven and defending it against naval counter-attack.

Personality
A formerly lawful officer, Toramir grew to hate his former kingdom and was driven to madness and paranoia by a twenty-year-long of crime.
